### Page 2

宏观经济点评  
请务必阅读正文之后的信息披露和法律声明 2 
煤价格分别上涨 2.34%、2.69%；建材相关价格边际回升，熟料、全国水
泥均价和石油沥青价格环比分别上涨 7.90%、3.17%、9.09%，PVC 价格
上涨 3.09%，螺纹钢现货价格小幅上涨 0.36%。有色金属表现分化，铜价
环比上涨 0.27%，铝价下降 2.50%；新能源相关商品仍偏弱，碳酸锂价格
环比下降 22.33%，纯碱、光伏级多晶硅和热轧卷价格分别下降 2.92%、
0.60%、1.98%。终端食品价格整体偏弱，农产品批发价格 200 指数环比
下降 2.96%，猪肉批发价下降 3.64%，蔬菜和鸡蛋价格分别下降 8.53%、
12.57%；汽油、柴油市场价分别上涨 0.54%、5.08%。 
生产端结构分化，需求端仍受内需偏弱与假期扰动共同影响 。10 月，生
产端结构分化，长流程钢铁和部分化工行业偏弱，短流程钢铁、水泥发运
等指标边际改善；需求端受国庆假期影响较大，电影票房明显走强， 汽车
零售、快递和公路货运环比回落；地产销售同比继续走弱、二手房价格下
行，指向地产修复基础仍不牢固。价格端看，能源、化工和建材价格回
升，农产品价格继续下降，显示当前价格修复更多由上游工业品推动，尚
未形成向终端需求和消费价格的广泛传导。往后看，需重点观察假期扰动
消退后生产和物流等能否恢复，以及上游价格上涨能否在需求改善的配合
下形成更持续的价格修复。 
风险提示：国内稳增长政策推进不及预期， 地产市场修复不及预期，海外
需求超预期回落，能源及大宗商品价格波动超预期，地缘政治冲突加剧。

1. 生产端 
1.1 开工率 
截至 10 月第四周， 高炉开工率 （247 家） 为80.01%， 环比下降0.49 个百分点； 电
炉开工率为 64.10%，环比下降 0.99 个百分点；冷轧开工率为 83.02%，环比持平；螺
纹钢开工率为 35.73%， 环比下降2.75 个百分点； 磨机运转率为39.92%， 环比下降2.28
个百分点；浮法玻璃开工率为 66.75%，环比持平；石油沥青装置开工率为 23.90%，环
比上升 5.75 个百分点；PVC 开工率为 61.47%，环比下降 0.63 个百分点；纯碱开工率
为 66.55%，环比上升 2.89 个百分点；PTA 开工率为 69.01%，环比上升 2.04 个百分
点；全钢胎开工率为 57.79%，环比上升 0.16 个百分点；半钢胎开工率为 64.78%，环
比持平。

1.2 库存 
库存方面， 螺纹钢库存为147.21 万吨， 环比下降3.39%； 冷轧库存为129.50 万吨，
环比下降 0.99%；热卷库存为 360.40 万吨，环比下降 2.24%；铁矿石港口库存为
16,462.55 万吨，环比上升 0.14%；焦煤库存（247 家钢厂）为 761.14 万吨，环比下降
0.42%； 煤炭重点电厂库存可用天数为25.20 天， 环比增加9.57%； 水泥库容比为59.22%，
环比下降 0.22 个百分点； 水泥发运率为41.32%， 环比下降4.20 个百分点； 浮法玻璃库
存为 7,051.90 万重量箱，环比下降 0.72%；纯碱企业库存为 136.71 万吨，环比下降
7.88%；沥青仓库库存为 31,870 吨，环比持平。

2. 需求端 
2.1 地产 
截至 10 月第四周， 地产方面，30 城商品房成交面积为 172.90 万平方米， 环比增长
10.21%；100 城土地成交占地面积为 1,917.75 万平方米， 环比下降51.44%； 成交土地
溢价率为 1.39%，环比上升 0.72 个百分点。二手房挂牌量指数全国为 0.70，环比下降
6.64%；一线城市为 0.07，环比持平；二线城市为 0.44，环比下降 2.22%；三线城市为
1.01，环比下降 9.82%。二手房挂牌价指数全国为 139.72，环比上涨 0.04%； 一线城市
为 170.34， 环比下降0.27%； 二线城市为126.09， 环比上涨0.09%； 三线城市为120.72，
环比下降 0.01%。

投资评级说明 
投资建议的评级标准  评级 说明 
评级标准为报告发布日后的 6 个月内公司股价 （或行业
指数） 相对均值基准指数的相对市场表现。 其中A 股市
场以沪深 300 指数为基准； 新三板市场以三板成指 （针
对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针对做市转让标的）
为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准。 
股票评级 
买入 相对均值基准指数涨幅在 10%以上 
增持 相对均值基准指数涨幅在 5%~10%之间 
中性 相对均值基准指数涨幅在-5%~+5%之间 
减持 相对均值基准指数跌幅在 5%以上 
行业评级 
看好 相对表现优于市场 
中性 相对表现与市场持平 
看淡 相对表现弱于市场

Document 010: 11月2日两场座谈会点评：小资金大乘数：稳市工具制度化如何改变定价分母

Source PDF: all report pdf/11月2日两场座谈会点评：小资金大乘数：稳市工具制度化如何改变定价分母.pdf
Report source: 山西证券
Date: 2026-11-03
Pages: 3
Status: ok

### Page 1

——11 月 2 日两场座谈会点评
2026 年 11 月 3 日 策略研究/事件点评
相关报告：
山证策略团队
分析师：
赵晨希
执业登记编码：S0760521090001
邮箱：zhaochenxi@sxzq.com
事件描述：
 两场座谈会同步落地：央行、金监总局、证监会于 11 月 2 日先后召开
“证券、基金、保险公司互换便利座谈会”和“股票回购增持再贷款座
谈会”。证监会副主席刘浩凌、央行副行长邹澜、金监总局副局长丛林
出席，23 家证券基金公司、26 家银行参会。
 两项工具“从应急走向制度化”：（1）互换便利（SFISF）：余额 1050
亿元，第三批扩容至 31 家券商/基金公司，申请额度超 1000 亿元，11
月 3 日第三次 SFISF 操作实际落地 1000 亿元，31 家机构参与，中标费
率 10bp，期限 1 年可展期，后续将“适当增加参与机构、降低互换费率、
优化质押模式”。（2）回购增持再贷款：签约约 4400 亿、累计发放超
2800 亿；由阶段性安排转为长期制度性安排，再贷款利率从 1.25%降至

1%。
 证监会答记者问的四条政策线：（1）工具端：引导权重指数公司、龙头
科技股做出标志性回购案例，督促已披露计划加速实施。（2）产品端：
18 只主动 ETF 募资超 350 亿（16 只触达 20 亿上限），加快建仓；继续
推权益类 ETF、股债恒定比例多资产指数基金、浮动费率基金。（3）两
融端：研究优化融资保证金比例、折算率、维持担保比例，适度释放融
资空间，盘活存量流动性。（4）回购规则：要求年度分红划出比例纳入
回购专项基金、超募资金强制部分回购、护盘式回购收益闭环用于注销。
投资要点：定价机制改变，而非资金本身。政策通过降低回购成本、提供机
构流动性支持、优化杠杆管理参数三条路径，系统性降低了权益资产的持有
成本和交易摩擦。政策影响的量化特征呈现“小资金、大乘数”结构，三项
工具可动用资金规模占自由流通市值仅约 1.42%，但通过成本压降、EPS 增
厚、PE 扩张三条乘数路径，潜在影响规模可达数万亿级别，同时两融松绑
作为弹性增强器可改善存量资金的使用效率，提升短线风险偏好。

 规模维度：政策工具属催化剂而非主要增量资金。政策工具来看，互换
便利余额 2050 亿元，占 A 股自由流通市值约 0.43%；回购增持再贷款
签约约 4400 亿，占 A 股自由流通市值约 0.92%，其中累计发放超 2800
亿，占 A 股自由流通市值约 0.58%；主动 ETF 募资 350 亿元，占 A 股
自由流通市值约 0.07%。三项工具可动用规模合计约 6,800 亿元，占全
A 流通市值约 1.42%。量级上可见政策工具并非可直接托举指数的体量，
而是通过压低风险溢价和交易成本来撬动市场自身定价，属于催化剂而
非主要增量资金投放。
 成本维度：再贷款降息的财务影响。再贷款利率从 1.25%降至 1%，存
量 2800 亿对应年利息成本从 35 亿元降至 28 亿元，年省 7 亿元，降幅
20%；若增量 1600 亿（签约 4400 亿元−已发 2800 亿元）全部按 1%投

### Page 2

放，年成本仅 16 亿元。4400 亿元全量按 1%，年总成本 44 亿元，比旧
利率省 11 亿元/年。对单家回购公司，成本下降直接转化为回购的净收
益，1%的利率已低于多数高分红公司的股息率，回购注销的套利空间
从接近中性变为明确正收益。
 盈利维度：回购注销对 EPS 的增厚。用公式 EPS 增厚 ≈ 回购额/市值
÷ (1−回购额/市值) 测算，假设温和情景回购占市值比为 0.6%（略高于
2800 亿元占全 A 市值比）、中性情景回购占市值比为 1%、积极情景回
购占市值比为 1.4%，EPS 增厚分别约 0.60%、1.01%、1.42%，回购额
占全 A 归母总净利（假设 Q3 线推）比分别约为 8.78%、14.63%、20.48%，
回购额整体不超过年利润，并未构成对存量现金的消耗，高 ROE 和强
现金流的公司为这一机制的受益者。
 杠杆维度：两融保证金比例调整的弹性。从保证金比例、折算率、维持
担保比例三个维度同时研究优化，表明监管层有意释放存量融资空间的
效率。融资保证金比例下调后，存量保证金可撬动千亿资金。以 2026

年 10 月 30 日实际融资余额 23078.12 亿元为基准，按监管通用公式推导，
若保证金比例从当前的 100%下调至新比例 95%、90%、80%，理论增
量分别可达 1214.64 亿元、2564.24 亿元、4072.61 亿元，考虑参与率、
风险偏好、集中度等因素，实际增量亦可达百亿元，潜在杠杆资金是短
期弹性的主要来源，也是波动放大的潜在根源。
 机构维度：互换便利的扩容效应。叠加存量展期余额 1050 亿元，全市
场可参考的互换便利支持池约 2050 亿量级。31 家机构分享 1000 亿新增
申请，均值约 32.3 亿元/家，若全额操作，占全 A 日均成交额（假设积
极情景约 1.8 万亿）的 5.56%，10bp 互换费率下，1000 亿年互换费用仅
约 1 亿元，利润影响可忽略，信号意义远大于成本意义，工具已完成从
应急到常态化的定位切换。单家 32.3 亿元的流动性额度，对头部券商而
言是“低成本流动性保险”， 对中型机构是打破融资分层的关键通道。
机构维度的价值不在利润弹性，而在降低系统性被动抛售概率。

 估值维度：盈利底与政策底的乘数效应。政策工具通过压缩流动性折价、
治理不确定折价、消除尾部风险预期压缩分母端风险溢价，撬动整个市
场估值中枢上移的乘数效应。全 A 总市值 116.45 万亿，三季报全 A 归
母净利润 5.10 万亿，隐含 PE 14.63 倍，PE 每扩张 1 倍，对应市值增量
7.96 万亿，相当于当前总市值的 6.83%。这是最关键的量化锚点：政策
工具压缩的是风险溢价，直接体现为 PE 扩张，14.63 倍的隐含 PE 为这
一扩张提供了估值空间，而 7.96 万亿的每倍 PE 弹性说明政策的边际影
响在估值端远大于在资金端。
风险提示：政策执行不及预期；盈利下修对冲估值扩张的风险；海外流动性
收紧与外资风险偏好外溢；两融杠杆双向放大与结构性脆弱；回购注销执行
质量与制度套利风险；盈利预期与政策工具落地节奏潜在乘数效应影响等。

• 10月S&P Global綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖較前月回落，但仍處高檔區間；服務業動
能略為放緩，製造業PMI則升至56.0，創近4年半新高。新訂單增速加快 ，顯示企業需求、投
資及庫存回補動能仍具韌性 ，然而企業售價與投入成本同步上升 ，反映需求改善 、供應鏈延
遲及成本轉嫁效應持續存在 ，通膨降溫進程仍具不確定性 。整體而言 ，市場關注焦點轉向通
膨與利率走勢，高利率環境恐維持較長時間，進而推高股債資產評價波動。
中國
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季 GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期 ，在出口與科技製造業帶動下 ，
短期成長可望維持穩定 ，但若內需未能有效改善 ，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散 ，建議聚焦在
半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈 ，及高端製造相關產業 ，至於消費 、房地產及傳統內需類股 ，

仍需觀察後續刺激政策與消費信心回升情況。
法國
政局動盪
• 法國金融市場因各地抗議活動持續擴大 ，面臨股債雙跌的局面。市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支
出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮 。法國10年期公債殖利率一度突破 5%，法德利差亦擴
大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標仍處於歷史相對低檔 ，遠低於歐
元區主權債務危機期間，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務危機的風險仍相對有限。

美國PMI維持強勁擴張，經濟成長續航但通膨壓力再升溫
• 企業需求強勁支撐美國景氣維持高檔擴張 ：美國10月綜合PMI初值由58.4降至57.6，雖略低於前月，但仍處於近五年來
的高位區間，顯示企業活動持續高速擴張；其中服務業 PMI為57.9、製造業PMI升至56.0並創53個月新高，新訂單增速
更創逾4年半最佳表現，反映內需穩健、出口需求改善與企業補庫存效應同步發酵，美國第四季經濟動能仍具韌性。
• 成本上升與供應瓶頸推升通膨風險： 企業售價漲幅創2022年8月以來最高，能源、運輸、金屬原料及薪資成本同步上揚 ，
加上供應鏈交期延長與訂單積壓擴大，使企業得以持續轉嫁成本，暗示未來數月消費者通膨可能再度偏離聯準會目標區
間，市場對利率維持較高水準的預期恐因此升溫。
• 後市觀察：景氣韌性有利獲利成長，但通膨可能推升殖利率、壓抑高評價資產。投資配置建議優先布局美國大型科技股 、

美國金融股及工業資本支出受惠族群，可透過NASDAQ 100 ETF、S&P 500 ETF及美國銀行股ETF參與企業獲利成長；
固定收益則以短天期投資級債為主，以降低利率波動風險。
資料來源：Bloomberg， 2026.10.24

海外資金流入美國市場，支撐美國資產吸引力
• 海外資金持續流入美國：美國財政部8月TIC資料顯示，
整體資金淨流入 1,998億美元，其中民間部門淨流入
1,785億美元，為主要資金來源；外資淨買進美國長
期證券3,007億美元，遠高於美國投資人加碼海外資
產216億美元，反映全球資金在經濟成長與流動性考
量下，對美國金融市場仍維持高度的配置需求。
• 全球資金偏好美國資產： 外資 8月淨買入美國股票
1,319億美元，高於美國公債與公司債 ，顯示海外投
資人仍看好美國企業獲利與經濟基本面 ，雖然短期國
庫券持有量下降，部分跨境美元資金亦出現波動，但
以10月近況來看長天期公債標售仍吸引境外央行 、
保險資金及大型資產管理機構參與，反映高殖利率水
準對長期資金仍具吸引力。在經濟成長維持韌性與企

業獲利支撐下，美國資產仍為全球資金配置的重要核
心市場。
• 市場看法：海外資金流入有助反映美國資產吸引力，
惟後續仍須觀察通膨 、Fed政策及企業獲利表現 。投
資布局建議可以美國投資等級債、美國中短天期公債
及大型AI基礎建設受惠族群為核心配置 ，兼顧收益、
防禦及長期成長潛力。

歷史數據顯示全球股市突破歷史新高後，後續上漲機率高
• 很多人直覺以為見高就是頂，但過去20年的統計資料顯示，全球股市在創下新高後，多數時間選擇繼續向上走。
• 勝率顯著偏高： 全球股市 (MSCI全球指數 )創新高後持有一年 ，獲得正報酬的機率高達 62%，三個月後的正報酬機率更達
71%。美股與台股表現最亮眼，標普500指數創高後一年上漲機率為76%，那斯達克為79%；而台灣股市表現更為驚人，創
高後三個月內上漲的機率高達82%。
• 下檔拉回風險有限：在創高後短期(一個月內)修正幅度其實相當溫和 ，全球多數主要市場的回徹幅度中位數僅 1%～2%左右。
• 突破前高優於連續創新高： 若市場在歷經至少一個月以上的整理後突破前高 ，其未來一年的平均報酬率高達 8.4%，遠高於

連續每天創新高的3.9%。這代表市場經適度整理後的突破，具備更健康的上漲動能。

市場寬度收窄，由少數巨頭獨撐
• 當前的市場特徵由少數巨頭帶頭衝： 科技股權重較高的指數(如那斯達克、台股、韓股)，長期以來都有少數權重股拉動指數
的現象。自2024年以來，全球13個主要市場中有10個市場在創新高時，參與上漲的股票比例明顯下降 。已開發市場創新高
時高於200日均線的股票比例從過去 75.5%降至65.1%，新興市場更降至 61.3%。這意味著指數看似風光 ，但多數個股並未
同步創高，當漲勢過度集中於少數大型科技巨頭時，整體市場的抗風險能力會相對減弱。
• 小結與投資建議：歷史經驗顯示，創新高本身是強勢市場的特徵，後續繼續走揚的機率遠大於立刻崩盤。然而，鑑於目前市
場寬度收窄、權重高度集中於科技股的現狀 ，盲目追高個股的風險已顯著增加 。投資建議可以利用ETF掌握大盤趨勢，由於

指數上漲主要由龍頭股驅動 ，透過市值型ETF(如追蹤標普500ETF)能直接享受龍頭巨頭帶來的指數創高紅利 ，避免選錯個股
而賺了指數、賠了價差。此外，提防短期股市回檔，可運用定期定額分批布局或等待大盤適度拉回整理再行加碼，降低追高
風險。

法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓
• 法國政局動盪加劇財政壓力，股債同步承壓：法國各地抗議活動持續擴大，最初由學生發起，隨後因生活成本高漲而引發其
他群體響應。在財政空間有限、俄烏戰爭與美伊衝突推升能源價格的背景下，政府難以有效緩解民生成本壓力，法國金融市
場因此面臨股債雙跌的局面。
• 公債殖利率攀升，但系統性風險仍相對可控：市場憂心法國政府可能擴大支出以平息抗議，加劇財政可持續性疑慮。法國10
年期公債殖利率一度突破5%，法德利差亦擴大至歐債危機以來高點。不過，歐洲央行系統性壓力綜合指標（CISS）仍處於
歷史相對低檔，遠低於歐元區主權債務危機期間，顯示金融市場尚未出現廣泛的系統性壓力，短期內演變為新一輪主權債務
危機的風險仍相對有限。

日本央行本週料維持利率不變
日本央行利率走勢預估
資料來源： Bloomberg，2026.10.15 資料來源： tradingeconomics，2026.10.21
日本貿易收支
• 日本貿易逆差擴大，進口增幅高於出口：日本2026年9月貿易逆差擴大至5,366億日圓，較去年同期的2,777億日圓增加93%，
高於市場預期的1,810億日圓，連續第五個月呈現貿易赤字。出口年增16.7%至10.97兆日圓，受惠於半導體相關電子零件、
製造設備及汽車出口成長；惟進口年增18.9%至11.51兆日圓，增幅高於出口，主要受到原油、半導體電子零件及液化天然
氣進口增加帶動。其中，原油進口年增51.1%，部分反映日本在荷姆茲海峽局勢緊張下，積極分散能源採購來源。
• 日本央行本週料維持利率不變：日本央行9月將政策利率調升25個基點至1.25%，創31年來新高。考量前幾次升息對經濟的

傳導效果仍待觀察，加上貿易逆差擴大反映進口成本壓力，多數分析師預期日本央行本週將暫停升息，以評估貨幣緊縮對經
濟與通膨的影響。

中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期
• 中國國家統計局公布第三季GDP年增率4.5%，優於市場預期：顯示中國經濟在第二季落底後呈現溫和回升 ，從季度表現來看，
一、二、三季增速分別為5.0%、4.3%及4.5%，反映政策支持與外需復甦開始發揮效果 ，全年達成官方4.5%成長目標的機率
明顯提升，第三季經濟改善的主要動能來自出口 ，受惠於全球企業持續擴建 AI基礎設施，及科技產品需求強勁 ，中國高科技
產品出口表現亮眼 ，尤其是積體電路 、伺服器、電腦零組件，及儲存設備等產品增幅顯著 ，高技術製造業維持雙位數成長 。
同時，中國對美國及歐盟出口維持強勁 ，推升貿易順差 走升，為支撐經濟的重要力量 。然而，內需疲弱仍是當前經濟最大隱
憂，房地產調整尚未結束，居民消費與企業投資意願保守，使經濟復甦結構呈現「外熱內冷」的特徵。

• 展望後市：在出口與科技製造業帶動下 ，短期成長可望維持穩定 ，但若內需未能有效改善 ，經濟成長動能恐難全面擴散 ，建
議聚焦在半導體、AI硬體、伺服器供應鏈 ，及高端製造相關產業 ，至於消費、房地產及傳統內需類股 ，仍需觀察後續刺激政
策與消費信心回升情況。
資料來源：開源證券，2026.10.22

彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高
• 彭博預估騰訊、阿里巴巴在中國AI競爭中勝出機率較高：彭博產業研究認為，中國AI產業未來將由大型科技平台主導 ，其中騰
訊、字節跳動與阿里巴巴憑藉龐大用戶基礎 、數據資源、資金實力及完整生態系統 ，在AI競賽中具備明顯優勢 ，這些企業可將
AI廣泛應用於電商、社群、遊戲、金融與雲端業務，提升營運效率並強化競爭門檻，例如：阿里巴巴利用 AI 驅動電商平台上的
個人化產品推薦，而騰訊則運用 AI 優化短影音廣告並強化微信功能。騰訊自 2026 年 7 月起開始將混元 AI 模型整合至其電子
遊戲中，使遊戲角色及助理能更準確地回應玩家 ，然而，AI模型訓練與基礎建設投入龐大 ，獲利能力短期仍受壓抑 ，且須面對
監管、資料隱私及晶片供應等挑戰 。此外，智譜、月之暗面、MiniMax及商湯等AI新創雖具技術創新能力，但缺乏規模效益、

穩定現金流及長期資本支持，未來獲利挑戰較大。
• 投資建議：看好中國AI長期發展者，可優先關注具資金 、算力與應用場景優勢的大型平台企業 ，受惠程度可望高於多數 AI新創
公司。
資料來源：彭博，2026.10.21

台灣9月外銷訂單再創新高，台股第四季有望先蹲後跳
• 台灣9月外銷訂單再創新高： 9月外銷訂單1,142.1億美元，連兩個月破千億美元 ，再創單月新高；年增率高達 62.4%，連續
20個月呈雙位數正成長 。經濟部表示，AI強勁需求超乎預期 ，預估今年第四季接單還會優於第三季 。展望10月表現，外銷
訂單將持續亮麗，估落在1,125億至1,159億美元，有望連三個月破千億美元。
• 台股第四季有望先蹲後跳： 10月初以來，台股在盤中觸及歷史高點 49,968點後面臨一波修正 ，主要反映在新一輪的財報公
布前，市場觀望AI投資是否存在泡沫化風險 。短期來看，台股在季線附近出現支撐 ，不過在11月初的美國期中選舉落幕前 ，
台股維持區間震盪的可能性較高；中期來看，美國期中選後往往因政治不確定性下降，歷史統計股市上漲機率高，加上台灣

年底集團作帳及選舉行情，正向看待年底台股表現。
資料來源：經濟部統計處，兆豐銀行整理，2026.10.20。

1. 本資料內容僅供內部同仁及客戶參考，非作為任何金融商品或服務之購買或銷售之推介，不得視為買賣有價證券或其他金融商品之要約或要約之引誘。
2. 本資料提及之經濟走勢預測不必然代表基金之績效，基金投資風險請詳閱基金公開說明書。
3. 基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益 ，投資人申購
前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 本資料所載內容如涉及商品資訊 ，商品相關資訊仍以經核可之條款及文宣 (DM)為主，本行網站已備有公開說明書可供查閱基金應負擔之費用及相關投資風險 。投
資人申購基金係持有基金受益憑證，而非本資料提及之投資資產或標的。
5. 本資料所載內容取自本公司相信可靠之資料來源，且為特定日期之判斷，本公司不保證其即時性、精確性及完整性，亦不提供或嘗試遊說客戶做為投資之判斷依據。

資料內容邇後若有變更，本公司將不做預告或主動更新。投資人於決策時應審慎衡量本身風險，如因信賴本資料內容而做出或改變投資決策，須自行承擔結果，本
公司及本公司所屬員工，均不負任何損害賠償及其他法律上之責任。
6. 本資料內容之著作權屬兆豐銀行所有，禁止就所載資料任何形式之抄襲、引用或轉載。
7. 本資料所載部分基金涉及非投資等級債，投資人投資以非投資等級債券為訴求之基金不宜占其投資組合過高之比重 。基金經金管會核准，惟不表示絕無風險。由於
非投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，故本基金可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支
付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損。部分基金不適合無法承擔相關風險之投資人。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良
管理人之注意義務外不負責本基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
8. 基金年化配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除息日前一日之淨值×一年配息次數×100%。「年化配息率」為估算值。當月配息率計算公式：每單位配息金額 ÷除

息日前一日之淨值×100%。
9. 基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動。投資人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績
效亦不代表未來績效之保證。本資料如以基金過去績效進行模擬投資組合之報酬率時，僅為歷史資料模擬投資組合之結果，不代表本投資組合之實際報酬率及未來
績效保證，不同時間進行模擬操作，其結果亦可能不同。
10. 部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之相關費用。基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部份，可能導致原始投資金額減損。各基金由本
金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至各投信基金公司及總代理人網站查詢。

本次報告：電話訪談

鴻海 (2317 TT) 
上修獲利效益預期

永豐觀點 
垂直整合能力，維持 AI 伺服器領先地位，營收規模擴大，上修獲利效益預期。 
 投資評價與建議 
研究部維持鴻海買進建議：鴻海集團於科技日展現垂直整合、精密加工能力，並憑
藉著規模化交付能力， 預期於伺服器市場維持四成以上市占率 、並能優先取得新世
代產品參考設計， 營收規模放大的同時體現獲利率效益， 獲利金額擴大以及營運資
金運用，並樹立組裝產業進入門檻。研究部參考近期公告之財務及產業資訊，預期
鴻海 2H26~2027 年營運具上修空間 ， 調整後預估鴻海 2026 年/2027 年稅後 EPS 
18.79 元/25.46 元。惟近期總體利率環境影響股價評價，目標本益比從 15 倍調整
至 14 倍，維持鴻海買進建議，目標價 356 元(14x2027FEPS)。

ESG 評析 
鴻海企業永續評鑑整體為 A 等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 A 等與 A 等。

### Page 2

2

鴻海為專業 ODM/EMS 廠商，產品涵蓋手機、伺服器、平板、網通設備等終端產
品，以及各類電子零組件如連接器、連接線等，旗下重要子公司包含工業富聯
(601138 SH)、富智康(2038 HK)及鴻騰精密(6088 HK)，近年公司開始積極投入研
發資源在電動車 、 數位醫療 、 機器人 、AI、 半導體 、5G 等領域 。 主要客戶為Apple、
HP、Dell、Sony、Cisco、北美四大 CSP 等。2Q26 產品營收占比：消費暨智能
29%、雲端網路 51%、電腦終端 15%、元件及其他 5%。 
鴻海科技日展現垂直整合能力  統合零組件到機櫃生產：(1) 鴻海科技日強調展現
垂直整合能力及精密加工能力，提供客戶 one stop shop，垂直整合、設計製造服
務。鴻海具備伺服器 Level 1 到 Level 12 的生產能力，從螺絲、零組件、機構件
等至伺服器系統整機櫃。鴻海團隊從產品設計開始即整合組裝、良率、服務零組件
到整系統最佳化的解決方案。(2) 鴻海協助輝達、Google 打造 AI 工廠、AI 基礎
建設，以自動化縮短資料中心建造時程。(3) 施耐德電機、東元提供電力設備，與
鴻海進一步合作打造模組化資料中心。 4. 本田與三菱電機展示與鴻海在電動車未
來自動駕駛、駕駛監控系統、 ECU 等合作方向。 (5) 鴻海同時推出新電動車平
台,Model A+ 、K 系列。A+為休旅車，其特色為取消 B 柱讓車內寬敞，並將配置 
L4 自動駕駛感測器。Model K 為輕型卡車，提供短程 120 公里、長程 250 公里車
型選擇。 
展場上鴻海集團展出 AI 伺服器相關產品，1. 與 Vertiv 合作液冷基建的 AI Data 
Hall 解決方案。(2) 展出 NVIDIA DSX POD 解決方案，以及 Vera Rubin 世代 
Compute Tray、Switch Tray、CPO Switch、CPU Tray、LPU Tray 等相關產品。
(3) Google 下一代 TPU 將使用的 OCS 全光交換機。(4) 在 NVIDIA CPO Switch
使用 的精 密 光學 對位 機 構 件、FAU 光纖 連 接產 品。(5) 集團 公司 鴻 騰精密
(6088.HK)， 展出液冷相關零組件QD、Manifold； 電力相關零組件Busbar、Power 
Whip；主板上連接器 Paladin、SOCAMM 等。甚至下一代 Vera Rubin Ultra 將
採用 ZQD、POGO 連接器也已取得 NPI。 
美系大廠資本支出 2027 年上修至 6 成以上增長 建置需求未歇：上週美股法說會
後，統計 Bloomberg 美系五大 CSP 加上 Space X，2026 年資本支出從年增率從
分別從 102%上修至 103%，2027 年從 44%上修至 60%，2026 年及 2027 年資
本支出金額分別達 8,793 億美元及 1.41 兆美元，顯示AI 算力建置需求仍未停歇。 
上修 3Q26、4Q26 營收獲利預估：鴻海先前公告 3Q26 營收達 3.0 兆優於預期，
以及子公司工業富聯公告 3Q26 財報優於預期。以此推算鴻海營收擴大規模效益
帶來獲利表現上修，研究部預期營益率增長至 3.8%，稅後 EPS 上修至 5.10 元。
上修 4Q26 營收預期，主因3Q26 營運基期墊高，以及iPhone 維持出貨動能、GB
機櫃出貨量出貨量、Vera Rubin 機櫃單價跳升等，推算帶動營收季增 14%，稅後
EPS 上修至 5.85 元。

營運現況與分析

### Page 3

3

AI 伺服器需求為鴻海營運增長動能：AI 伺服器需求主要由美系 CSP、AI 模型商及
Neocloud 驅動，鴻海 GPU AI 伺服器除了原本美系 CSP 需求，也將有新型雲端
客戶開始貢獻。研究部預期鴻海維持 VR200 的主要供應商角色，整體市占率維持
達 40%以上，近期上修預估整體市場 Vera Rubin 至約 64,000 櫃 NVL72 (市場預
期為 80,000~90,000 櫃)，同時鴻海 2027 年將增加美系 CSP AI ASIC 伺服器機櫃
供應，為 2027 年營運增長動能，鴻海在提高市占率、營收規模放大的同時體現獲
利率效益、獲利金額擴大以及營運資金運用，並能樹立組裝產業進入門檻。 
綜合以上，上修後預估 2026 年營收 11.1 兆，年增 37%，毛利率 6.0%，營益率
3.7%，稅後淨利 2,627 億，年增 39%，稅後 EPS 18.79 元。2027 年營收 15.5 兆
元，年增 39%，毛利率 5.4%，營益率 3.4%，稅後淨利 3,555 億元，年增 35%，
稅後 EPS 25.46 元。

### Page 1

3Q recap - strong op leverage offset GPM pressure — 3Q26 net profits (+125% y/y) 
topped our/BBG estimates 35%/28% respectively owing mostly to stronger sales 
and larger degree of op leverage. Sales reached a record NT$32.4bn (+24% q/q and 
+83% y/y), 23%/12% above Citi/BBG estimate, which can be attributable to strong 
demand in edge AI, semi equipment and smart healthcare applications. GPM fell to 
36.7% (-1ppt q/q and -2.2ppt y/y) below Citi/BBG estimates of 38.0%/37.6%, 
reflecting higher component prices. Nevertheless, OPM expanded to 20.9%, 
underpinned by strong op leverage on higher sales scale. OP profits +151% y/y beat 
Citi/BBG forecast 33%/21%, the key driver to the earnings beat.    
Implications — Strong order intake is encouraging with 3Q BB ratio reaching 1.6x 
and NA particularly robust at 2.1x. However, we believe the stock's rich valuation 
limits further re-rating potential, particularly as a higher comparison base could 
moderate growth in 2027E. Continued GPM compression also reinforces our 
concerns over material/component inflation and the timing of price increases to 
offset higher costs. Advantech will host 3Q26 results call on Nov 4th. We will revisit 
our numbers and share key takeaways following management's updates.

Advantech
Valuation
Our target price of NT$670 is based on 35x 2H26-1H27E EPS, at the high-cycle of the stock's valuation range between 20-35x
since 2020, reflecting solid BB ratio, improving margin profile and ROE.
Risks
Key upside risks that could cause Advantech’s shares to trade above our target price include: 1) better-than-expected
marcoeconomic outlook; and 2) better-than-expected demand and margin profile. Key downside risks would include: 1)

slower-than-expected demand recovery, especially the China market; and 2) further deterioration of demand in US and EU
markets.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.

call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in

published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is
moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day

period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be
different from and does not affect a stock’s fundamental equity rating, which reflects a longer-term total absolute return

Document 109: 261029_daiwa_RTK

Source PDF: all report pdf/261029_daiwa_RTK.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-29
Pages: 5
Status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: On 29 October, Realtek reported 3Q26 results that missed our estimates but 
were in line with the consensus. This was mainly due to weaker-than-expected revenue 
likely on a deeper demand rebalancing despite pricing power on supply-chain cost 
passthrough. While 4Q26 outlook looks intact, management expects steady topline growth 
into 2027, supported by a multi-year bandwidth upgrade cycle, continued pricing resilience 
and emerging AI opportunities from cloud to edge to physical AI, despite likely still subdued 
PC and consumer electronics demand. We rate the stock Outperform (2) premised upon 
our key thesis of a multi-year/multi-G (gigabits per second) bandwidth upgrade cycle 
across wireline and wireless to benefit Realtek, which is further echoed by management’s 
2027 outlook.

Highlight 
• 3Q26 results miss on lower topline. Realtek reported 3Q26 net profit of TWD4.1bn, 
missing our estimate by 7% while in line with the consensus. With financial metrics such 
as margins broadly in line, the miss was mainly due to lower-than-expected topline 
which came in 8% below our estimate at TWD37bn. We believe the weak revenue was 
driven by a deeper demand rebalancing after customer restocking and pre-stocking in 
1H26 on seasonality and supply-chain uncertainties, flagged in our note, Downgrading: 
pricing to bridge rebalancing but …, 4 August. Although Realtek raised prices to pass on 
cost inflation to the supply chain, it failed to offset such rebalancing. As usual, 
management gave no quantitative guidance for 4Q26 but expressing “prudent and 
steady” revenue run rates, in line with our expectations. We will be reviewing our model 
in due course.

• Key takeaways. At today’s results call, management further expected a steady topline 
growth heading into 2027, driven by: 1) multiple-G bandwidth upgrade cycles across 
wireline and wireless networking, including Wi-Fi 7 and 8, multi-G Ethernet, PON and 
switch, to enhance product mix with market-share gains adding to upside; 2) likely 
sustained pricing power with the company continuing to pass on inflated costs to the 
supply chain; and 3) despite likely still subdued demand from PC and consumer 
electronics markets, the ongoing development of AI, from cloud to edge AI to physical AI 
applications, is creating growth opportunities and tallying with our investment thesis of 
the multi-year/multi-G bandwidth upgrade cycle to structurally benefit Realtek.

Recommendation 
We rate Realtek Outperform (2). The stock price has generally consolidated around 
TWD670-790 over the past 3 months due to lack of catalysts amid its demand rebalancing, 
suggesting patience for investors. Key downside risk to our call would be any commodity 
price hikes leading to worse-than-expected demand rebalancing.

2026.10.29   
HD현대중공업  (329180)

3Q26: 조선사는 우직해야 한다  Company Brief

3분기 매출 5조 7,536억원/영업이익 8,992억원 
HD현대중공업의 2026년 3분기 매출은 5조 7,536억원/영업이익은 8,992억원으로 
컨센서스(6조 1,295억원/9,671억원) 및 당사 추정치(6조 859억원/9,679억원) 대비 
매출 및 영업이익 각각 -6.1%/-7.0%, -5.5%/-7.1%를 하회하는 실적을 기록하였다.  
조선사업부 매출이 4조 3,988억원/영업이익 6,463억원(OPM 14.7%)이며 이중 상선 
OPM은 14.7%로, 2분기 17.8%에 비해 3.1%P 하락하였으나 1분기 15.9%와 크게 
차이나지 않는 수준을 유지하면서 조업일수 및 환율 하락에도 불구하고 P-MIX및 
빈티지 개선에 따른 효과를 보여주었다. 해양사업부는 매출 3,799억원/영업이익 
1,265억원(OPM 33.3%)로 지난 분기(15.9%)보다도 훨씬 높은 영업이익을 
기록하였다. 엔진기계사업부는 매출 9,511억원/영업이익 2,299억원(OPM 24.2%)를 
기록하며 역시 견조한 실적을 기록하였다.  
일회성 이익으로 Trion 공사의 C/O 180 억원, 일회성 손실로는 셧다운에 따른 
조업감소 영향 500억원 및 환율하락에 따른 이익 감소분 450억원이 있었으며 금액은 
공개되지 않았으나 임단협 잠정타결에 따른 성과급/타결금 지급에 대한 추가 충당금의 
75% 인식이 반영되었다. 알려진 일회성만 제거하더라도 실질 영업이익률은 
17%이상으로 2분기 16.4%보다 높아진 호실적이라고 할 수 있다. 특히 RUYA 공사가 
본 궤도에 오르면서 해양사업부의 이익률이 괄목할 만큼 높아졌으며 지속가능하다는 
회사의 설명이다.

투자의견 BUY 및 조선업종 TOP PICK 유지, 목표주가 530,000원 하향 
HD현대중공업의 목표주가를 530,000원으로 38.3% 하향하지만, 투자의견 BUY와 
조선업종 TOP PICK 을 유지한다. 환율 하락 및 28년 하반기 엔진증설 매출확대, 
해양공사 이익률 상승 등 요인을 종합 반영하여 실적 추정치를 미세 반영하여 도출한 
2027F BPS 131,910원에 Target P/B를 기존대비 하향한 4.0배로 적용하였다. 특수선 
기대감 및 선가상승 기대감 하락에 따른 조선업종 전반의 밸류에이션 하락을 반영하여 
Target을 낮췄지만 그럼에도 조선업종 중 가장 높은 멀티플을 부여하며 Top 
Pick의견을 견지한다. 여러 악재에도 불구하고 HD현대중공업은 탄탄하다. 본업인 
상선사업부의 9월말 기준 수주량은 137.9억 달러로 목표치를 이미 초과 달성하였으며 
전사 기준으로도 수주목표 90.5%를 채워넣었다. 수주잔고 또한 상선 421억 
달러/전사 609억 달러로 각각 작년 말 대비 12.6%/13.2% 증가하였다. 해양부문과 
엔진기계부문은 조선사업부보다도 높은 이익률로 든든한 실적의 기반이 되고 있다.  
최근 HD현대그룹은 미국의 EPC강자 Kiewit과 미국 합작법인 설립에 관한 계약을 
맺고 그린필드 기반의 미국 신규 조선소 건설을 추진하고 있다. 빠르면 올해 중으로 
건립과 관련한 세부 사항이 공개될 예정이며 HD현대중공업이 중추적인 역할을 담당할 
것으로 보인다. 일찍부터 브라운필드 방식으로 접근한 동종사와 달리 다소 시간은 
걸렸지만 과감하게 결정된 투자다. 조선소 예상 준공 역시 2028년 이후로 예상되어 
당장 기업가치에 반영하기는 어려우나 엔진 증설과 함께 2028년 후에는 분명한 실적 
상향 요인이 될 것으로 예상한다. 우직하게 본업에 충실하며 신중하게 미래를 준비하는 
조선소, 귀감이다.

본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

종목추천 투자등급

 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상

1
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
AUSTRALIAN DWELLING
APPROVALS BULLETIN
Units lift the headline
2 November 2026
Key points
• Total dwelling approvals rose 5.1%mth
in September, although the annual pace
moved into contraction at –0.8%yr with
the detail suggesting the underlying
cycle is starting to turn down.

• The headline increase was driven by
another volatile rise in private unit
approvals (+16.6%) which more than
offset a significant decline in private
detached house approvals (–5.3%).
• Units drove gains in NSW and
Queensland. WA, SA and Victoria all
recorded declines. Detached dwelling
approvals fell across all states.
• The total value of residential approvals
fell 2.8%mth but a sharp rise in non-
residential approvals – led by the
data centres – saw the total value of

building approvals rise 13%mth.
• Interest rate increases, weakness in
established housing markets, rising costs
and pessimistic consumer sentiment
are likely to remain headwinds.
Total dwelling
approvals
+5.1%mth,
–0.8%yr

### Page 2

the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
Total dwelling approvals rose 5.1%mth in September, while
the annual pace moved into contraction at –0.8%yr. The
result was well above both Westpac’s expectation for a
7.0%mth decline and the market expectation for a 2.0%mth
rise. Unit approvals continued to drive headline volatility,

surging in September, while detached dwellings, which
provide a better read on the underlying trend, recorded a
sizeable decline.
Note that most figures in the charts and table are presented on
a rolling-three month basis to smooth out monthly volatility.
Private detached dwelling approvals fell 5.3%mth in
September, unwinding August’s upwardly-revised 5.4%mth
rise (+1.7ppts). Recent resilience has kept the segment
elevated, with approvals remaining above 10,000 and the

annual pace at 9.6%yr. However, the quarterly profile provides
a clearer read on the underlying trend and suggests detached
dwelling approvals are beginning to respond to recent interest
rate increases (see here). Approvals rose just 0.9%qtr in
September vs 3.1%qtr in June and 5.9%qtr in March.
Private unit approvals remained volatile, rising 16.6%mth in
September following 21.7%mth decline in August. Strength
was led by high-rise approvals, while low-mid-rise approvals

recorded a more modest increase. Despite the monthly rise,
the annual pace fell further into contraction at –20.5%yr.
Among the states, NSW recorded the strongest result,
with private approvals rising 35.3%mth led by a surge in
private unit approvals. Queensland also recorded an increase
(+9.3%mth), while WA (–2.9%mth), SA (–3.5%mth) and
Victoria (–29.0%mth) all declined.
In value terms, residential building approvals fell 2.8%mth,
driven by a 3.3%mth decline in new residential approvals,

partially offset by a 0.8%mth rise in renovation activity.
Non-residential approvals once again reversed sharply, rising
45.9%mth, as the data centre-dominated ‘other commercial’
segment recorded an 8-fold increase on a non-seasonally
adjusted basis. The total value of building approvals rose
13.0%mth in September. On a non-seasonally adjusted basis,
the value of building approvals rose 8.3%mth overall, but
declined 3.0%mth when excluding the ‘other commercial’

segment.
September’s result reinforces the importance of looking
through headline volatility in unit approvals. While total
approvals rose in the month, detached dwelling approvals
slowed on a quarterly basis and fell across all states, pointing
to softer underlying momentum. The annual pace of total
approvals also moved into contraction for the first time
since January. Interest rate increases, the sharp downturn
in established housing markets and rising costs are likely to

weigh on further growth, with pessimistic consumer sentiment
an additional headwind (see here).
Detached approvals point to softer momentum

Key points
• The Cotality home value index – covering
all of Australia – fell 1.2% in October
following a 1.3% decline in September
and 1.4% falls in August and July.

Annual growth has turned negative,
running at –2.8%yr with prices down
6.7% from their peak in March.
• Across the major cities, Brisbane and
Perth led the declines in the October
month, recording falls of 2.0% and

remains more pronounced in Sydney

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The Cotality home value index, covering all of Australia, fell
1.2% in October with slight revisions to previous months
again shaving a further 0.3% off prices. The correction
has now seen prices decline 6.7% from their March peak

with annual growth firmly in negative at –2.8%yr. Turnover
continues fall as well, down 22.5%yr. Housing-related
tax policy changes, interest rate rises and heightened
uncertainty have triggered a sharp contraction in markets.
The downturn is now comparable to the previous two
housing market corrections. Nationally, the downturn in
2022-23 saw prices decline 7.5% and turnover fall 29% peak
to trough. The 2017-18 correction saw prices fall 8.6% and

turnover by 24%, with larger falls in Sydney and Melbourne.
Note that unless specified, figures in titles, tables and
commentary are in non‑seasonally adjusted terms, consistent
with the ‘headline’ figures reported by Cotality. However,
figures shown in charts are in seasonally adjusted terms.
Preliminary estimates of turnover show rolling 3mth volumes
down 5.6%qtr in October, previous estimates again revised
lower. The monthly pattern suggests declines re ‑accelerated

through September‑October after stabilising somewhat in July ‑
August. The RBA's interest rate rise in late September and a
further expected tightening likely contributed to the shift.
The detail shows Brisbane and Perth are now leading the
way in terms of monthly price declines although corrections
to date remain significantly larger in Sydney and Melbourne
while smaller capitals and regional areas continue to see much
milder downturns. Across price tiers, all segments are now

showing similar rates of decline after top price tiers had seen
a more pronounced and earlier downturn. 'Top tier' segments
have tended to lead recent price cycles.
Looking across jurisdictions,
• Sydney prices dropped 1.6% in October having averaged
similar‑sized declines over the previous five months. While
all segments and tiers continue to record falls, the last few
months has seen the pace of decline moderate a touch in the
top tier segments that have led cycles in the past, although

they are still a long way from stabilisation.
• Melbourne prices declined 0.8%mth, in line with
September's move and continuing the slower pace of
retreat. As noted, this likely reflects the weaker starting
point coming into the correction, prices already at 5½ year
lows. Interestingly, the detail shows the moderation in price
declines in recent months has been more pronounced for
'top tier' units where prices are back where they were in
2016.
Housing downturn deepens

forecasts are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially
from these forecasts.
3WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
• Brisbane prices are tracking a very fast rate of decline
that is also seeing large downward revisions. Prices fall
2% in October with September and August now both
showing 2.1% falls, revised down from 1.5% and 1.6%

respectively (August having previously been estimated at
just 1%, July's move having seen a similar series of revisions
–1.2% to –1.8%). The combined effect has taken the rolling
3mth change to –6.1%qtr from –4.7%qtr last months and
–2.7%qtr in August and –0.6%qtr in July. At 0.4%yr, annual
price growth has virtually stalled flat having run at 18%yr as
recently as May. All tiers and segments are recording similar
rates of decline.
• Perth is seeing something very similar, prices dropping a

further 1.7% in October, with September's decline revised
down to 2.1% from –1.2% and August's to 2.0% from –1.7%.
At 3.9%yr, annual growth is still positive but off steeply
from the 24% pace back in May. As with Brisbane, all tiers
and segments are recording similar rates of decline.
• Adelaide dwelling prices posted a somewhat milder 1.2%
decline in October, , September's –1.3% decline marked
down to –1.7%. Annual price growth slowed to 3.7%yr,

similar to Perth's but with less of a whiplash from strong
gains to large declines. Turnover has also been much
steadier, sales holding flat over the last three months and
down just 12.4%yr.
• The smaller capitals were again more mixed, price growth
still positive in Darwin (0.6%mth, 9.7%yr), relatively resilient
in Hobart (+0.5%mth, 5.5%yr) but weaker in Canberra
(–0.7%mth, –3.7%yr).
• Regional markets showed a 0.9% decline in Qld but slightly
milder declines in NSW (–0.7%) and Victoria (0.6%). Annual

prices growth is in the 1.4 ‑3.5%yr range.
All up, the October updates make for bleak reading. With the
RBA widely expected to raise interest rates again, the near
term outlook remains very challenging. Next week's consumer
sentiment update will show how this is shaping housing ‑
related sentiment – so far, consumer price expectations have
weakened but stopped short of swinging to the clear negative
consensus that emerged in the 2022 ‑23 correction. That now

looks to be at risk. Either way, momentum looks set to remain
weak into year‑end and, barring a significantly improved
inflation and interest rate outlook, well into 2027.

Document 383: NZ labour market preview, Q3 2026 (2026-11-07)

Westpac New Zealand Economics
Turning point?
• We expect the unemployment rate to edge
down to 5.5% for the September quarter.
• Job numbers have been gaining momentum
in recent months, and now look to be enough
to outstrip population growth. However, the
participation rate will be the deciding factor.
• Despite cost-of-living pressures on workers,
the existing slack in the labour market is likely
to keep wage growth subdued.
• Our forecasts are a little more positive than

what the RBNZ assumed in their September
policy statement. Assuming they deliver on
an OCR hike this afternoon, the labour market
surveys could have an important bearing on
whether they follow up with another hike in
December.

The New Zealand economy has returned to growth over
the last year, despite a number of headwinds. That’s been
accompanied by some modest growth in jobs, though
to date it hasn’t been enough to prevent a further rise

in the unemployment rate. However, we think that next
Wednesday’s labour market reports could show the jobs
market reaching a turning point.
We expect the unemployment rate to ease from 5.6%
to 5.5% in the September quarter, which would mark
the first fall in almost five years. That’s not a firmly held
view, given the usual margin of uncertainty around this
ECONOMIC BULLETIN
Preview of Q3 labour market surveys:
4 November, 10:45am.
28 Oct 2026 | Michael Gordon, Senior Economist | +64 9 336 5670 | michael.gordon@westpac.co.nz

### Page 2

2
Westpac New Zealand Economics
measure. But we’re now seeing consistent evidence
that jobs growth is outstripping the modest pace of
population growth. We also note that the previous result
was 5.56% in unrounded terms, so it wouldn’t take much
to tip the rounded rate down to 5.5% this time.
On the whole, our forecasts are a little stronger than
what the RBNZ assumed in its September Monetary
Policy Statement. While our unemployment rate forecast

is identical, we’re more positive on both employment
and labour force participation. Meanwhile, our pick of a
0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index is slightly ahead of the
RBNZ’s estimate of 0.4%.
Assuming that the RBNZ delivers the OCR hike that we
expect this afternoon, we think they will be noncommittal
about the chances of another hike at the 9 December
Monetary Policy Statement. In that respect, the labour
market surveys could be important in setting market

expectations for the December review, as there aren’t
that many major data releases between now and then.
Forecast details.
We expect the Household Labour Force Survey (HLFS)
to show a 0.5% rise in employment for the September
quarter. That’s broadly in line with the Monthly
Employment Indicator (MEI), with the September release
this morning showing a third straight month of growth
in filled jobs. We note that employment growth in the
HLFS has actually outstripped the MEI for the last nine

quarters, but it’s not clear why this has been the case and
we’re wary of extrapolating it too far into the next quarter.

Employment growth of around 0.5% would be more than
enough to absorb the modest 0.3% rise in the working-
age population, and on its own would suggest a fall in

the unemployment rate. But it also comes down to the
rate of labour force participation, which we don’t really
have much guidance on. The participation rate has been
choppy over recent quarters, and in the June quarter
we saw an unusually large 0.3pt rise to 70.7%; we’re
assuming some payback this time with a more modest
0.1pt rise to 70.8%.
We’ll also be keeping an eye on the broader indicators of
slack in the labour market, although we don’t formally use

these in our forecasts. The June quarter saw a sharp rise
in the underutilisation rate from 12.9% to 13.8%. However,
we think there’s some residual seasonality in this series,
relating to one component in particular: people who are
available for work but not actively looking. In that case,
we could see a substantial pullback in the underutilisation
measure this time.
Previously we’ve characterised the labour market as a
“low-fire, low-hire” environment, with much of the rise in

unemployment reflecting the difficulty of breaking into
the workforce, rather than job losses. There are some
signs of a thaw emerging, with job vacancies picking up
from their lows over the last year and business surveys
pointing to a lift in hiring intentions. But we still expect
that it will be a long road to recovery – our current
forecasts see the unemployment rate at 5% by the end of
next year.
While cost-of-living pressures are again rearing their

head as a major concern for workers, the existing degree
of slack in the labour market will continue to make it
difficult for them to push for bigger pay increases. We
expect a 0.5% rise in the Labour Cost Index (LCI) for
the September quarter, which would nudge the annual
growth rate up slightly to 2.1%. That said, we don’t have a
lot to go on for this forecast. The MEI includes a measure
of median weekly earnings, which has shown no sign of
acceleration so far – but this is only a very loose indicator

of wage pressures, as it will also reflect any changes in
the composition of jobs and pay rates.

Report Introduction
Our proprietary dbDataInsights survey shows cyber threats are evolving faster than organisations can adapt
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.
“It’s the only thing that can shut down my business.”
That was the punchline from a conversation I had recently with the CEO of a large
privately held financial services company. This was his comment when I followed up

as to why cybersecurity risk is the number one concern that “keeps him up at night”.
To help understand this risk, our dbDataInsights team surveyed more than 50
cybersecurity experts. The uncomfortable conclusion is that many firms are
unprepared for cyber threats. Cyber risk is increasing as AI adoption accelerates.
—89% agree the threat landscape is evolving faster than organisations can adapt.
—78% say the gap between highly and poorly prepared organisations is widening.

What has changed with AI advancement? Cyberattacks have become faster,
cheaper, more deceptive and more scalable. Capabilities once reserved for
sophisticated actors are now broadly accessible to a new generation of profit-driven
criminals. At the same time, rising geopolitical tension has made cyber operations
an increasingly attractive low-cost, deniable tool for nation states. Corporates and
investors therefore need to understand their broader exposure and critical

infrastructure risk in their supply chain and with stakeholders.
Among other autonomous incidents, the recent Hugging Face incident
highlighted AI’s cyberattack potential. AI agents caused harm without
direction from a human attacker, with weak guardrails and no human
judgement to restrain them. Beyond the systemic questions it raised, the attack
highlights three additional concerns: (i) AI operates at a speed and scale humans
cannot match, with agents taking over 17,000+ actions in four days; (ii) the intrusion

began in the data-processing pipeline, where AI platforms are uniquely exposed;
and (iii) the agents escaped their test environment through a widely used third-party
tool representing supplier risk in an unfamiliar form.

### Page 4

Report summary and notable takeaways
Secular trends and AI adoption have widened the attack surface; the severity of losses is compounding
Source: FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3), company security advisories, OECD, company reports, Deutsche Bank Research.
Secular trends have widened the attack surface. This includes rapid
digitalisation, widespread Internet of Things (IoT) endpoints, hybrid workforces,
cloud adoption and deeper supply chain dependencies. The scale of the threat

increase is reflected in data from the FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3),
which shows cybercrime complaints more than doubled from 2019 pre-pandemic
baseline of ~467,000 to ~1.0mn in 2025, with a 3x expansion relative to 2017 levels.
Barriers to entry are lower; attack tools are now sold “off the shelf”. Today,
cybercrime marketplaces, available on the dark web, look less like the underworld
portrayed in films and more like an online software store. Buyers can access AI-

powered tools that scan for software vulnerabilities (see top-right chart showing
reported CVEs after the release of cyber capable frontier models), write malicious
code at speed, and provide access to malware that rewrites its own code to avoid
detection as it runs. Ransomware-as-a-service (RaaS) takes it further, renting out
ready-made attack kits, often in exchange for a portion of the profits.
Impersonation is becoming harder to detect. Hackers can now generate more

realistic phishing messages, while voice cloning and deepfakes make impersonation
convincing enough to gain access unnoticed. With stolen credentials, attackers often
aren’t hacking in, they are simply logging in.
The rise of loose cybercriminal collectives. Some of the most damaging recent
attacks have been linked to loosely organised, often English-speaking networks such
as ShinyHunters and Scattered Spider. The latter has been widely named as the

group behind the 2025 Marks & Spencer cyberattack, in which the hacking group
deployed RaaS. AI is enabling these groups to become larger and faster, widening
the gap between the speed of attack and a defender’s response.
The severity of losses is compounding. A 2026 IBM study puts the average breach
at a record USD ~5.0mn, up 12% YoY, but the impact on large companies can run
into hundreds of millions or even billions. Estimates for global cyber insurance

premiums (per Munich Re) point to a rise from roughly USD 7.0bn in 2020 to USD
15.0bn in 2025, with expectations to reach USD 28.0bn by 2030.

### Page 5

5
Among the more concerning survey takeaways is that a large-scale and
significant systemic incident is now the base case.
— 83% expect a significant systemic attack within two years and 81% expect it to be
moderately, very, or extremely severe for economic activity and markets.
— 81% say AI favours attackers today, with not one respondent putting defenders
ahead of attackers. Only 20% expect defenders to be ahead in five years.
Geopolitical tensions coupled with rising cyber capabilities have transformed

cyberspace into a digital battlefield between global powers. Intent has moved
towards espionage and disruption of not only critical infrastructure (e.g., healthcare,
finance, power, industrial systems, defence) but private companies as well. Chinese
President Xi Jinping has linked cybersecurity to national security and economic and
social stability, with China’s public statements similarly emphasising the protection
of critical infrastructure. At the same time, the US’s June 2025 Executive Order on

Cybersecurity reflects its continued focus on national cyber resilience.
Europe relies on US cyber tools while its start-ups lag in funding. Reliance on US
security vendors exposes European firms to potential access, update or export
restrictions over the long-term. Shrinking this gap will prove difficult given US cyber
start-ups attract nearly 6x the venture and growth funding of European peers. And
while the timing of quantum computing (which could break widely used encryption

methods) remains uncertain, it could further deepen Europe’s dependency.
Ultimately, cyber preparedness is a financial variable. Investors should ask what
firms spend on security versus AI adoption, how well they know their vulnerabilities,
and how fast they can recover.
— Only 24% of respondents think organisations have sufficient visibility into the
threats relevant to them, even though 57% saw cybersecurity budgets rise over
the past 12 months.

— 81% agree that cybersecurity related laws and regulation cannot keep pace, and
65% doubt leadership understands cyber risk well enough.

### Page 6

6
When cyber weakness = share price consequence…
Investors are more aware of cyber risk today, but they rarely sell a stock due to
perceived cyber vulnerabilities (although we are aware of some who have).
—What is the potential impact? Our dbDataInsights event study of 47 breaches
shows a cumulative breach-attributed return of c. -2.9% within the first two
trading days and c. -6.0% through first 20 days trading days. The erosion then
continues more gradually, reaching c. -6.7% nine months after disclosure (see

slide 13).
Which industries are most vulnerable?
—Our survey respondents point to (1) government & public sector (52%), (2)
financial services (50%), (3) energy and utilities (46%) and (4) healthcare and life
sciences follow (39%, rising to 48% among US respondents).
We should caveat, that high sector exposure does not mean every company in it is
equally vulnerable. Some firms in these “exposed” sectors may very well be better
prepared given they have built specialist teams and defence protocols for decades.

What about access to credit and ratings? Credit and rating providers are paying
attention. Moody’s and S&P both cut their outlook on Jaguar Land Rover within two
months of its 2025 hack. Today, this type of impact is not widespread. But if risk
escalates through a systemic incident or an AI-driven step change in attacks these
mechanisms, including ESG ratings, could reprice asset ownership or impact access
to credit. In fact, some lenders are already pricing it. Journal of Corporate Finance

research using US syndicated loan data found borrowers with persistently higher cyber
risk pay 4–13bp more.
The bottom line? While a broader systemic attack is hard to prepare for, corporates
and investors should be asking questions about vulnerabilities, security investment,
preparedness and recovery should an attack occur. This is especially true for
industries quickly adopting AI capabilities and / or a high threat landscape.

dbDataInsights Cybersecurity Expert Survey methodology
—This survey was conducted among 54 cybersecurity professionals across the US
and Europe in September of 2026. Questions took the form of either multiple

choice selections or free form responses. The dbDataInsights team received the
responses anonymously, but respondents were required to work or consult in
cyber security roles
—Who took the survey? On self-identified roles, around 80% sit inside an
organisation’s cybersecurity function, with the remainder working independently
(15%) or in academia and research (6%). The most common titles were security
architect (23%), Chief Information Security Officer (16%) and security analyst (9%),

alongside Head of Security, Deputy CISO, Incident Response Lead and Security
Operations Lead.
—Only a small minority of respondents work for cyber resellers, thus survey views
are primarily attributable to those working within an organization on
cybersecurity.
—Survey participants were also asked for their perspectives on cyber resilience and
governance, what is misunderstood, alongside emerging threats, risk to AI
adoption and the influence of geopolitics.

—While responses were anonymous, in some cases we conducted follow up
interviews with certain participants where contact details were made available.
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research.

### Page 10

10
Risk from AI-enabled attacks and added AI adoption vulnerabilities
Insider threats and supply chain compromises remain a growing concern
“The fear of AI writing undetectable malware is … overblown. Attackers were
never short of malware, since they can buy it ready made along with initial
access. Good detection looks at behaviour rather than exact code, so a rewritten
payload still has to do the same suspicious things.”
“Frontier AI models are currently the main hype. Automated phishing and

scams are underappreciated, yet they are responsible for the bigger
monetary losses at the moment.”
“There are too many organisations relying on the same providers for
critical services and this means single points of failure.”
“A corporation might be very secure but if they rely on a third party that
does not have the same level of security posture, [it] could be
compromised via this third-party (basically their weak link).”
“Existing employee login behaviour and usage of AI is underappreciated –

it’s what poses the biggest threat to a breach.”
“No company can guarantee they won’t be breached, so there needs to
be more focus put on the ability to recover and survive an attack.”
Source: dbDataInsights survey of cybersecurity experts, September 2026; Cybersecurity Survey 2026, Deutsche Bank Research .

### Page 11

11
Experts believe governments are not keeping pace, and no one owns AI oversight
How experts surveyed by dbDataInsights view governance and regulation
—AI adoption is outpacing internal controls. As one expert put it: “Companies
are pushing the use of AI more to their employees without the proper
mechanisms and policies in place to prevent misuse or attacks initiated by the AI
systems themselves.”
—Regulation is struggling to keep pace. 81% of respondents agree that

cybersecurity-related laws and regulations are failing to keep up with
technological change, and 54% say regulatory requirements can improve security
but may also slow innovation. One respondent highlighted “the levels of
uncertainty, the long running nature of the problems, and the rapidity of change.”
—Responsibility for AI oversight remains unsettled. International bodies are the
leading choice, supported by 50% of respondents. No option commands a

majority, underscoring the lack of consensus on who should oversee AI
development.
—Better board questions may not guarantee better decisions. The survey
points to more informed questioning by corporate leadership, yet 65% doubt that
leadership teams understand cyber risk well enough to make informed business
trade-offs. Measuring activity more effectively than outcomes further complicates
oversight.
—Accountability is becoming personal. Only 43% say accountability for cyber risk

is clearly defined in their governance framework, yet the consequences now reach
the top as a handful of CEOs have left or gone into early retirement after
financially consequential cyber breaches.
Who should primarily oversee AI development?
dbDataInsights survey – September 2026, based on overseeing development while managing cyber risk

Material cyber breaches can cause a notable drop in share price
Our dbDataInsights study found a 2.9% drop on average within two trading days, with the cumulative breach-attributed return of -6.0% on trading day 20.

Source: dbDataInsights, Bloomberg Finance LP., Deutsche Bank Research, Company filings. Event study of reported cybersecurity incidents, 2015 -2025, 34 US and 13 European companies across nine sectors.
What typically happens to a company’s shares when a breach is reported?
—Short-term impact: The market prices the breach immediately. Our model shows
cumulative breach-attributed (i.e. isolating only the part of the share price move
we can pin on the breach itself) returns are c. -2.9% within two trading days of

at one year, although the one-year point estimate (our single best-guess figure)
should be treated with caution given the width of the confidence band.
—Pre-breach price behaviour: We find no evidence of information leakage ahead
of disclosure. While the breach component for the [-10,-1] window (ten trading
days before the news broke) is +0.04%, the corresponding t-stat value of 0.03 falls
notably below the usual bar for significance (c.2). As such, we conclude the market

had not begun reacting before the announcement.
Event study methodology
—Sample: 47 publicly reported cybersecurity incidents across the US and Europe
over 2015-2025, measured against each company’s regional sector benchmark.
Requirement of 5 years of stock returns pre-attack and 1 year of post-attack.
—Day 0: the day an incident was first publicly reported or disclosed.
—Baseline: We use five years of pre-incident share-price performance to
estimate: (i) Beta (β): how sensitive a company’s returns are to movements in its

sector and (ii) Alpha (α): the extent to which the company typically outperforms
or underperforms its sector after adjusting for that sensitivity.
—Attribution: post-incident returns are split into sector-relative returns, alpha
(α) and breach (abnormal returns attributed to the disclosure).
—95% confidence interval: Range within which we can say with 95% confidence
that the real share price impact lies, based on our sample data.
Total return attribution

95% Confidence Interval Breach impact negative at 95% confidence Cumulative impact of cyberincident

What happened?
A breach via an attack on a connected 3rd
party forced the suspension of all online
orders and led to empty store shelves.
What did it cost?
~£136mn of direct costs related to the
attack, estimated group operating profit
impact of around £300mn for the FY. M&S
also said they had received £100mn via
cyber insurance.
Coupang (Reported Nov 2025)
What happened?
A data breach exposed the records of
more than 30mn users with a former

employee reportedly behind the attack.
What did it cost?
The firm was fined more than ~625bn won
($409mn) (2/3rd directly attributable to
data leak), and also announced a $1.2bn
voucher compensation scheme.
UnitedHealth (Reported Feb 2024)
What happened?
Hackers reportedly used stolen credentials
to access a subsidiary’s (Change
Healthcare) systems, stealing data and
deploying ransomware.
What did it cost?
Total costs exceeded $3bn, with the CEO
summoned to testify before Congress,

where he confirmed that a $22mn ransom
was paid.
Equifax (Reported Sep 2017)
What happened?
A data breach saw hackers expose
sensitive customer information, including
145.5mn Social Security numbers and
more than 200k credit card numbers.
Attackers reportedly moved inside Equifax
systems for 76 days before being detected.
What did it cost?
Settlements, fines and legal costs
exceeded $1bn, while the firm also spent
more than $1.5bn on tech upgrades

following the incident.

Notable cyber attacks: how they happened, estimated costs and share price reaction, cont.
Nike (Reported Jan 2026)
What happened?
A cybercrime group leaked what it claimed
was 1.4TB of internal Nike data (appearing
to cover metrics such as product
specifications and pricing), following expiry
of a ransom deadline.
What did it cost?
While Nike did confirm it was investigating
the incident, it did not report any
operational disruption. Underperformance

shown in the chart does not appear to be
related to the breach.
T-Mobile (Reported Aug 2021)
What happened?
An attacker reportedly targeted an
exposed router as an entry point, before
using brute force attacks to gain further
access to T-Mobile systems.
What did it cost?
In 2022 the firm agreed to pay $500mn to
settle a class-action lawsuit ($350mn
payment to class members  plus $150mn
on security spend) but has since suffered
further data breaches, contributing to an

additional $31.5mn regulatory settlement.
Coinbase (Reported May 2025)
What happened?
Attackers bribed at least one support
agent at a connected 3rd party to access
Coinbase support systems and steal
customer data, then tricked users into
sending crypto.
What did it cost?
Coinbase refused to pay the $20mn
ransom demanded by the attackers to
keep the attack quiet. Instead they
reimbursed affected users and cited costs
of up to $400mn. The stock impact was

short-lived, though MSCI applied an ESG
score deduction.
Jaguar Land Rover (Reported Sep 2025)
What happened?
Production was shut down for five weeks
due to what was deemed a ransomware
attack; though no ransom was asked for
(later reporting suggested potential Russia
links were being investigated).
What did it cost?
JLR posted £485mn Q2’ FY26 loss (vs.
£398mn profit in Q2’ FY25), with an
additional £196mn in “cyber-related costs”.
These impacts filtered through to the

share price of JLR’s Parent (Tata Motors),
while UK economic impacts were
estimated at £1.9bn.

### Page 16

16
Targets go beyond tech and can also impact critical infrastructure
MGM Resorts (Reported Sep 2023)
What happened?
A vishing call to the IT help desk was reportedly used to reset credentials, after which
ransomware took down hotel systems, bookings and casino floor operations for several
days.
What did it cost?
MGM declined to pay a ransom. Around $100mn hit to third-quarter 2023 results plus
roughly ~$10mn of additional one-time expenses related to the incident.

Caesars Entertainment (Reported Sep 2023)
What happened?
The same social-engineering group as in the MGM incident targeted an outsourced IT
support vendor, gaining access to the loyalty programme database and member data.
What did it cost?
Caesars paid roughly $15mn of a reported $30mn demand and kept customer-facing
operations running.
NHS / Synnovis (Reported Jun 2024)
What happened?
Ransomware against pathology provider Synnovis significantly reduced its capacity to

process tests leading to delays / cancellations of over 11,000 appointments.
What did it cost?
Estimated 2024 direct operational financial impact of around GBP 32mn for Synnovis,
several times the profit reported in the prior year, plus wider NHS disruption costs.
Colonial Pipeline (Reported May 2021)
What happened?
A compromised VPN credential without multi-factor authentication allowed attackers to
access the network where they subsequently deployed ransomware. The operator shut the

pipeline down, triggering fuel shortages (c. 70% of gas stations in some states ran out of
fuel) across the US East Coast.
What did it cost?
A $4.4mn ransom was paid, of which about $2.3mn was later recovered by the US
authorities.
Bitget (Reported Sep 2026)
What happened?
Attackers exploited a vulnerability in a 3rd party security product to steal internal network
credentials, forge withdrawal instructions, bypass risk controls and ultimately steal crypto.

What did it cost?
An estimated $388mn worth of funds was transferred out by the attackers. While the firm
reimbursed affected users via its User Protection Fund, there were significant outflows
following the incident as users moved to take funds off the platform.
Progress Software (Reported May 2023)
What happened?
Attackers exploited a zero-day SQL injection vulnerability in Progress Software’s MOVEit file
transfer system. This was then used by attackers to steal data from firms running the

Debbie Jones | dbDataInsights | Under Attack | October 2026

Cyber breaches can impact consumer behaviour
Roughly 4 in 10 consumers we surveyed say they would stop buying from a company they shop with after a known breach or attack.
Source: dbDataInsights monthly consumer survey, Deutsche Bank Research. EU Average includes France, Germany, Italy and Spain.
How will customers respond to a data breach once it occurs?
dbDataInsights survey of consumer responses, % of respondents
How will customers react once the breach is resolved?

Consumer responses among those who would either stop buying or were unsure, % of respondents
Consumer surveys suggest that cyber breaches can undermine customer
loyalty.  Results of our dbDataInsights Consumer survey show the potential
mindset of a consumer after a cyber breach occurs:
—Across the US, UK and EU average, 39–41% say they would stop buying, while only
27–28% would continue. Around a third are unsure.
—Among those who would stop buying or are unsure, only 18–22% say they would

return as normal. Another 30–35% would buy selected products only, 17–20%
would not return, and 28–29% remain unsure. Note that this implies a range of
13-15% believe they would not shop at the store again after the breach is
resolved.
Admittedly, these stated intentions may overstate actual switching, particularly for
essential purchases where alternatives are limited. Nevertheless, the findings
suggest that cyber breaches that put customer privacy at risk can influence

purchasing decisions, especially if they have alternatives.

dbDataInsights Consumer Survey Methodology
—Our proprietary survey conducted in 2026 (January – September) asked

consumers how they would respond if a company they shopped at regularly
was involved in a data breach or cyberattack, and how they would respond
once the disruption was resolved. This monthly survey covers households and
consumers in Germany, France, Italy, Spain, the United Kingdom, and the
United States.
—For each country referenced above, a nationally representative sample of more
than 500 households is surveyed each month (over 600 households in the US).

Collectively, this meant over the course of the survey (January – September
2026), we surveyed almost 30,000 households.

### Page 18

18
Material cyber incidents have contributed to negative credit outlooks
The infrastructure exists for cyberattacks to have a greater impact on credit quality should cyber disruption continue to accelerate
Source: S&P, Moody’s, Fitch, Deutsche Bank Research.
—Credit rating agencies consider both pre-breach resilience frameworks and
post-breach financial impacts. Agencies assess pre-breach cyber preparedness
through a lens of mitigating risk, while post-breach credit impacts centre on

financial capacity to withstand the resulting shock (considers scale and length of
the disruption); highly leveraged issuers with limited liquidity are seen as more
vulnerable. Negative outlook changes at JLR and Equifax illustrate the potential
credit impact. Fitch generally considers permanent reductions in cash generation
more credit-significant than temporary cash-flow delays.
—Ratings agencies are moving towards continuous monitoring. In 2021,

Moody’s invested $250mn in BitSight, which produces daily security ratings.
Moody’s has since built BitSight’s ratings into its credit tools, including Credit
Catalyst and EDF-X, for uses such as underwriting and ongoing monitoring of
borrowers and suppliers. This means lenders can see a company’s exposed
vulnerabilities as a potential indicator of corporate cyber risk.
S&P Global:
“The increasing frequency of cyberattacks and the potential for rapid deterioration in credit

profiles after an attack are risk factors relevant for our rating assessments.”
Moody’s:
“Cyberattacks can undermine the creditworthiness of companies, organizations and
governments, across all sectors and geographies, hitting revenues, increasing costs, and eroding
customer trust.”
Fitch Ratings:
“Although cyber events cannot be predicted with great accuracy, the associated risk can be
managed through proactive strategies, technology, policies, and vigilance.”

“The chief executive officer and board of directors hold ultimate responsibility for managing
cyber risk.”
Example rating actions where cyber was a named factor
Outlook changes due to operational disruption, financial cost and reporting delays

Cyber governance & breaches can impact ESG scores
Sectors that hold the most personal data, and monetise it most directly, carry the greatest risk weighting in mainstream ESG scores
Source: MSCI ESG Ratings Methodology, Morningstar Sustainalytics ESG Risk Ratings, SASB Materiality Map, Deutsche Bank Resear ch.
Cybersecurity is generally seen as both a social and governance consideration
MSCI and Morningstar Sustainalytics both assess data privacy and security through management of the

risk, exposure to it and the controversies that follow a breach. Weak governance weighs on the score,
and a breach can cut it directly – more severe controversies draw larger deductions that persist for
longer.
Materiality varies sharply by sector. SASB flags data security as material across most consumer,
financial, healthcare and technology industries, with customer privacy concentrated in the most data-
intensive ones (see right).

### Page 20

IV. Investment, risk and opportunity

### Page 21

21
Europe’s dependency on US tech is firmly in the spotlight
Access to frontier AI models is now a security question; the funding gap for Europe will be hard to close
Source: Tikehau Capital – Tikehau Capital Barometer of European Investment in Cybersecurity – 7th edition 2026, Bloomberg Finance LP, Deutsche Bank Research.
—Growing cyber risk creates increased dependency on US cyber tech. Growing
concern over the cyber capabilities of frontier AI models has made ensuring

access to such technology a national security priority. US dominance over the AI
tech chain (e.g. chips, model development, etc), its willingness to impose AI-
related controls / restrictions, and previous pressure against Europe over tech
regulations has amplified concerns that dependence on US tech represents a
strategic threat to European sovereignty.
—Funding is the structural challenge. US cybersecurity start-ups attract almost 6x
the funding of European peers, and US-domiciled firms dominate the major

indices – 89% of the Nasdaq CTA Cybersecurity index and 93% of Global X
Cybersecurity ETF (which seeks to track the performance of Indxx Cybersecurity
Index) by weight. Europe has also seen home-grown firms such as Darktrace
acquired by US companies, though this reflects broader market trends rather
than anything cyber-specific. Ultimately, the US has a greater exit strategy for
start-ups as well.
—Boosting homegrown solutions a focus, but also a risk. EU policymakers are

seeking to address the dependency; e.g. the EU’s Cybersecurity and AI Action Plan
includes “scaling up Europe’s AI capabilities for cybersecurity” as one of its three
objectives. However, there are also risks associated with this endeavour; as one
dbDataInsights survey participant put it, “for most European organisations, the
bigger near-term danger would be moving too fast to less capable providers.”
—The wider relationship adds to the exposure. A more transactional

international environment, alongside Europe’s dependency on the US for energy
supplies and defence support, compounds the risk. Recent US-EU diesel
negotiations serve as a pertinent example of this, whereby the US reportedly
considered a diesel export ban to compel a release of European diesel reserves.

### Page 22

22
Cybersecurity as an opportunity
Tailwinds include demand related to keeping operations secure with AI acceleration and consolidation tailwinds
Source: dbDataInsights, Deutsche Bank Research. Investors, defined as those who invest in stocks/ shares (either individually or through a broker/advisor) on a regular basis , were asked whether they perceived cybersecurity / data privacy as a risk or an opportunity for investors in 2026.
Broadly, DB’s company research view is that cybersecurity becomes even

more important in the AI era. Whereas cybersecurity has historically been viewed
as a blocker/drag on innovation and operational speed, it is now viewed as a
strategic enabler for secure AI adoption.
In particular, network security may see renewed growth after being viewed as a
more mature category. Rising AI usage should drive additional compute and data-
centre capacity, expanding the infrastructure requiring segmentation and
protection. Agentic workloads may also generate lower-throughput but more

frequent and persistent interactions across models, tools and data sources,
increasing the volume and complexity of network-level inspection.
Vendor consolidation has been a structural trend in cyber for some time, though not
necessarily linear. That said, the current environment may accelerate the
consolidation tailwinds as customers look to close security gaps that may arise from
a fragmented tool stack.
See further commentary from DB’s company research team here.

Our March 2026 “personal investor” survey showed a more positive view
among “ESG” investors. 58% in the US and 52% in the UK saw cybersecurity/data
privacy as an opportunity. Among all investors, US respondents favoured
opportunity over risk (46% vs 30%). UK views were more balanced (38% vs 40%).

### Page 24

24
Appendix A - Attack types
Top Attack types What it is How AI is helping attackers
Malware Software built to damage, disrupt or gain unauthorised access AI writes harmful software faster. Google has found malware that rewrites its own code while running to avoid detection, and criminal marketplaces selling AI malware-building tools.
Phishing Deceptive messages that trick people into giving up sensitive information Where AI’s effect is clearest: fluent, personalised lures without the old spelling and phrasing giveaways. Google says state-backed groups use AI to mass-produce them, and AI can fake voices and video.

Denial of service (DoS) Flooding a system so it cannot serve legitimate users Little reported evidence so far. AI can help find weak points and automate traffic floods, but the attack itself stays fairly simple.
Distributed denial of service
(DDoS)
The same attack launched from many
compromised devices at once
AI can accelerate the development and automation of attack tooling, while large-scale
botnets provide the distributed capacity behind hyper-volumetric DDoS attacks.

Man-in-the-middle (MitM) Intercepting or altering traffic between two parties Mainly indirect. AI-written phishing pages and deepfake voices make it easier to trick people into connecting through an attacker or approving fake sign-ins.
SQL injection Malicious SQL queries injected to manipulate backend database operations AI tools scan sites and spot flaws in database code faster, so less-skilled attackers can find weaknesses that once needed real expertise.

Cross-site scripting (XSS) Scripts injected into web pages to hijack user sessions As with SQL injection: AI speeds up finding vulnerable pages and writing the malicious code.
Zero-day exploits Attacking a software flaw before a patch is released A major new development. In May 2026 Google reported the first zero-day it believes was developed with AI. Its September 2026 report says the bigger effect is attackers misusing known flaws faster, before fixes land.

Advanced persistent threats
(APTs)
An intruder staying hidden in a network for long-
term
Google reports state-backed groups using AI across the whole attack, from target
research and lures to tooling and sifting stolen data.
Ransomware Encrypting data or locking device and demanding payment to release it NCSC expects AI to add to the global ransomware threat by lowering the skill needed to break in. AI also helps criminals find the most extortable data.

IoT exploitation Abusing weakly secured connected devices for entry AI can scan the internet for unpatched cameras, routers and storage devices and identify which flaws they carry.
Debbie Jones | dbDataInsights | Under Attack | October 2026
Source: Deutsche Bank Research, National Cyber Security Centre, Google Threat Intelligence Group, Cloudflare, FBI Internet Crime Complaint Center (IC3)

### Page 25

Appendix B - European vs US cyber regulation
Europe US Federal
Debbie Jones | dbDataInsights | Under Attack | October 2026
Regulation Key requirements
NIS2 Directive In force 2023, transposition deadline Oct-24. Covers 18
sectors (up from seven) and creates an EU vulnerability
database. In-scope firms must manage cyber and
supply-chain risk and notify authorities of significant
incidents. Management bodies must approve and
oversee cyber risk measures, with potential liability for

infringements.
Cybersecurity Act In force 2019. Establishes an EU-wide certification
framework for ICT products, services, processes and
managed security services, and made the EU
cybersecurity body ENISA permanent.
Cyber Resilience Act In force 2024, main obligations from 2027.
Manufacturers and retailers of products with digital
elements must deliver security by design, a defined
support period and security fixes within it, plus
mandatory vulnerability management and disclosure.

Digital Operational
Resilience Act (DORA)
In force 2023, applies from 2025. Financial-services
specific, covering own-firm and third-party risk. Requires
an ICT business continuity policy, regular resilience
testing and disclosure of major incidents, and gives
regulators oversight of critical ICT providers.
Cyber Solidarity Act In force 2025. Creates a European Cybersecurity Alert
System to detect threats and share cross-border
warnings, plus a Cyber Emergency Mechanism to boost

preparedness and response.
Regulation Key requirements
White House Accord on
Super Intelligence
(2026)
Emphasises self-regulation to ensure AI safety, with a call for
external evaluations and the possibility of formal regulation in
future. Not legally binding; signed by President Trump and
leading AI companies.
Executive Order on
Cybersecurity (2025)
Highlights risk from foreign actors and orders further action
on defending digital infrastructure, securing the most vital

and governance.
CIRCIA (passed 2022,
not yet in effect)
In-scope entities, focused on critical infrastructure, must
report covered incidents to CISA within 72 hours and
ransomware payments within 24 hours of payment.
Gramm-Leach-Bliley Act
(amended 2024)
Financial institutions must explain information-sharing
practices and safeguard customer data, running a security
programme covering risk assessment, service-provider
oversight and incident response.

Federal Information
Security Modernization
Act (2014)
Applies to federal agencies and contractors: system security
plans, continuous monitoring, incident response capability
and mandatory incident reporting.
Federal Acquisition
Regulation (cyber
provisions 2016)
Governs federal procurement, setting baseline cyber
requirements for suppliers so cyber risk is built into
government contracting. Agencies may impose stricter
provisions.
